p|N^{\prime}

\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}=w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}_{p}w^{\prime}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{-1}&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}

and set

(4.28)

f^{{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}}_{p}:\ x\in G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\mapsto f_{p}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}x\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}).

Remark 4.2
.

The introduction of this extra twist is absolutely crucial: without it, the spectral size of the relative trace formula would select the spherical vector in
\pi_{p}
, and the local period integral at
p
would vanish. See Remark
5.8
.

4.4.
Choice of the global test function

We then set

f=f_{\infty}.\prod_{p}f_{p},

(4.29)

f^{{\mathfrak{n}}}=f_{\infty}.\prod_{p|N^{\prime}}f^{{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}}_{p}.\prod_{p\nmid N^{\prime}}f_{p}.

Alternatively, if we set, for any place
v

(4.30)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{v}=\begin{cases}w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}_{p}w^{\prime}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{-1}&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}&\hbox{ if $v=p=N^{\prime}$ and }\\
w^{\prime}.w^{\prime}=\mathrm{Id}_{3}&\hbox{ if $v=\infty$ or $v=p\nmid N^{\prime}$}\end{cases}

and

\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}=(\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{v})_{v}\in G({\mathbb{A}}),

we have

(4.31)

f^{{\mathfrak{n}}}:x\mapsto f(\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}x\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}).

4.5.
Cusp Forms on
U(W)

In this section, we discuss the cuspidal representation

\pi^{\prime}\simeq\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}\otimes{\bigotimes}^{\prime}_{p}\pi^{\prime}_{p}

of
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})=U(W)({\mathbb{A}})
and the associated cuspform
\varphi^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}
.

We recall that we want
\pi^{\prime}
to have trivial central character, its archimedean component
\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}
isomorphic to the holomorphic discrete series of weight
k
, for every
p|N^{\prime}
its
p
-component,
\pi^{\prime}_{p}
is isomorphic to the Steinberg representation
\mathrm{St}_{p}
and for any prime
p
not dividing
N^{\prime}
,
\pi_{p}^{\prime}
is unramified.

Using the trace formula, one can show that such
\pi^{\prime}
exists (see below); alternatively one can construct
\pi^{\prime}
"explicitly" by functoriality: let
\pi_{1}
be a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL(2,{\mathbb{A}})
with trivial central character such that

-

\pi_{1\infty}\simeq\pi^{+}_{k}
is the holomorphic discrete series of weight
k
,

-

for every
p=N^{\prime}
,
\pi_{1p}
is the Steinberg representation,

-

for
p\nmid N^{\prime}
,
\pi_{1p}
is unramified.

Let
\pi^{\prime}_{E}
be the base change of
\pi_{1}
to
E
; it follows from
the works of Flicker and Rogawski (
[
Fli82
,
Rog90
]
) that
\pi_{1,E}
descent to an automorphic cuspidal representation of
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
with trivial central character which has the required local properties.

Indeed, because
\pi_{1}
is selfdual and
\pi_{1,E}
is by construction invariant under the complex conjugation
\sigma_{E}
, the representation
\pi_{1,E}
is conjugate dual:

\pi_{1,E}^{\vee}\simeq\pi_{1,E}\simeq\pi_{1,E}\circ\sigma_{E}.

Since
W
is even dimensional, it is sufficient to see that
\pi_{1,E}
is conjugate symplectic
[
GGP12
]
or in other terms that the automorphic induction

\operatorname{Ind}^{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{E}(\pi_{1,E})
is symplectic. We can verify this by considering the global
2
-dimensional Galois representation
\rho^{\prime}
attached to
\pi^{\prime}
and showing that the Galois representation

\operatorname{Ind}^{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{E}(\mathrm{res}_{E}(\rho^{\prime}))
is symplectic: its exterior square contains the trivial representation. We have

\operatorname{Ind}^{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{E}(\mathrm{res}_{E}(\rho^{\prime}))\simeq\rho^{\prime}\oplus\rho^{\prime}.\eta

(
\eta=\eta_{E}
is the quadratic character corresponding to
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
)
so that

\Lambda^{2}(\rho^{\prime}\oplus\rho^{\prime}.\eta)\simeq\Lambda^{2}(\rho^{\prime})\oplus\Lambda^{2}(\rho^{\prime}.\eta)\oplus\Lambda^{2}(\rho^{\prime})\oplus(\rho^{\prime}\otimes\rho^{\prime}.\eta)=1\oplus 1\oplus{\mathrm{sym}}_{2}(\rho^{\prime}).\eta\oplus\eta

since the determinant of
\rho^{\prime}
is trivial.

Conversely one can show using
[
Rog90
]
that any representation
\pi^{\prime}
can be obtained in that way.

4.5.1.
The automorphic form
\varphi^{\prime}

We choose
\varphi^{\prime}
to be the "newform" on
U(W)({\mathbb{A}})
corresponding to a pure tensor

\varphi^{\prime}\simeq\otimes_{v}\xi^{\prime}_{v}\in{\bigotimes}^{\prime}_{v}\pi^{\prime}_{v}

where

-

\xi^{\prime}_{\infty}\in\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}
is a vector of lowest weight
k
(ie. is multiplied by
e^{-ik\theta}
under the action of the matrix
\kappa(\theta)=\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta},1,e^{-i\theta})\in SU(W)(\mathbb{R})
),

-

for every prime
p
,
\xi^{\prime}_{p}
is invariant under the open-compact subgroup
K^{\prime}_{p}(N^{\prime})
defined below.

Regarding the last point we set

G^{\prime}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})=\prod_{p}G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}})=G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{fin}})\cap G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})

and define

(4.32)

K^{\prime}_{f}(N^{\prime})=\prod_{p}K^{\prime}_{p}(N^{\prime})\subset G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}})

where

-

If
p
split in
E
,

(4.33)

K_{p}^{\prime}(N^{\prime}):=\Big\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&0&b\\
0&1&0\\
c&0&d\end{pmatrix}\in G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}):\ c\in N^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\Big\}

-

If
p
is inert in
E
,

(4.34)

K_{p}^{\prime}(N^{\prime}):=\Big\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&0&b\\
0&1&0\\
c&0&d\end{pmatrix}\in G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}):\ c\in N^{\prime}\mathcal{O}_{E_{p}}\Big\}

-

If
p
is ramified in
E
,
K_{p}^{\prime}(N^{\prime})=G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

Remark 4.3
.

The subgroup
G^{\prime}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
is the stabilizer in
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{fin}})
of the hermitian
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-submodule generated by
e_{1}
and
\Delta^{-1}e_{-1}
,

L^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}_{E}e_{1}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{E}^{-1}e_{-1}\subseteq W;

In other terms
G^{\prime}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
is the closure in
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathrm{fin}})
of
U(L^{\prime})
the stabilizer of
L^{\prime}
in
U(W)({\mathbb{Q}})
.

4.5.2.
The exceptional isomorphism

To be more concrete we recall that we have an exceptional isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebraic group

\SU(W)\simeq\SL(2)

given by

(4.35)

\iota_{E}=\iota:\ \ \begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\SL(2)\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}a&0&-b\Delta\\
0&1&0\\
-c\Delta^{-1}&0&d\end{pmatrix}\in SU(W).

Under this isomorphism, the image of the full congruence subgroup
\SL(2,{\mathbb{Z}})
is given by

\iota(\SL(2,\mathbb{Z}))=SU(L^{\prime})=G^{\prime}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})\cap SU(W)({\mathbb{Q}})

and more generally, if
N^{\prime}
is a prime coprime with
D_{E}
, the image of the usual congruence subgroup of level
N^{\prime}

\Gamma_{0}(N^{\prime})=\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in SL(2,{\mathbb{Z}}),\ N^{\prime}|c\},

is

\displaystyle\iota(\Gamma_{0}(N^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\bigl\{\begin{pmatrix}a&0&-b\Delta\\
0&1&0\\
-c\Delta^{-1}&0&d\end{pmatrix},\ \begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in SL(2,{\mathbb{Z}}),\ N^{\prime}|c\bigr\}

\displaystyle=K^{\prime}_{f}(N^{\prime})\cap SU(W)({\mathbb{Q}}).

Given our automorphic form
\varphi^{\prime}:G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
as above let
\phi^{\prime}:{\mathbb{H}}\mapsto{\mathbb{C}}
be the function on the upperhalf plane defined by

(4.36)

\phi^{\prime}(z)=j(g_{\infty},i)^{k}\varphi^{\prime}(\iota(g_{\infty}))

for
g_{\infty}\in\SL(2,{\mathbb{R}})
such that
g_{\infty}.i=z
and for

j(g,z)=(\det g)^{-1/2}(cz+d)

be the usual automorphy factor on
\GL(2,{\mathbb{R}})^{+}\times{\mathbb{H}}
. The invariance of
\varphi^{\prime}
along with the strong approximation property for
\SL(2)
imply that the function
\phi^{\prime}
is a well defined holomorphic cuspform of weight
k
with trivial nebentypus and a newform of level
N^{\prime}
. Now using again the strong approximation property for
\SL(2)
one can construct out of
\phi^{\prime}
an automorphic form which (abusing notations) we denote

\varphi:\GL(2,{\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\GL(2{\mathbb{A}})\mapsto{\mathbb{C}}

of weight
k
, ie. which multiplies by the character
e^{-ik\theta}
under the left action of

\kappa_{\theta}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta&-\sin\theta\\
\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{pmatrix}\in\SO_{2}({\mathbb{R}})

and invariant under the center
Z_{\GL(2)}({\mathbb{A}})
and under the open compact congruence subgroup of level
N^{\prime}

K_{0,f}(N^{\prime})=\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\GL(2,\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}),\ c\in N^{\prime}\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}\}.

The automorphic form
\varphi^{\prime}
generates the representation denoted (abusing notations)
\pi^{\prime}
and is a new, lowest weight vector of it.

We assume that
\varphi^{\prime}
is
L^{2}
-normalized:

(4.37)

\langle\varphi^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime}\rangle=\int_{[G^{\prime}]}|\varphi^{\prime}(g^{\prime})|^{2}dg^{\prime}=1

where
dg^{\prime}
denote the Tamagawa measure. With this normalization one has for the classical form
\phi^{\prime}

(4.38)

\langle\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}\rangle:=\frac{1}{\vol(\Gamma_{0}(N^{\prime})\backslash{\mathbb{H}})}\int_{\Gamma_{0}(N^{\prime})\backslash{\mathbb{H}}}y^{k}|\phi^{\prime}(z)|^{2}\frac{dxdy}{y^{2}}=c_{E}

where

\vol(\Gamma_{0}(N^{\prime})\backslash{\mathbb{H}})=\int_{\Gamma_{0}(N^{\prime})\backslash{\mathbb{H}}}\frac{dxdy}{y^{2}}=\frac{\pi}{3}N^{\prime}\prod_{p|N^{\prime}}(1+\frac{1}{p})

and
c_{E}>0
depends only on
E
.

Consider its Fourier expansion

\phi^{\prime}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}e(nz)

where

(4.39)

a_{n}=a_{n}(\phi^{\prime}):=e^{2\pi n\Im\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(\tau+x)e^{-2\pi inx}dx.

Since
\phi^{\prime}
is a newform, we also have

(4.40)

a_{n}=a_{1}.n^{\frac{k-1}{2}}\lambda_{\pi_{1}}(n);

here for any integer
n
,
\lambda_{\pi_{1}}(n)
denote the
n
-th coefficient of the Hecke
L
-function
L(\pi_{1},s)
(normalized analytically); it satisfies Deligne’s bound

(4.41)

|\lambda_{\pi_{1}}(n)|\leq d(n)=n^{o(1)}

(
d(n)
denote the divisor function) and its first coefficient
a_{1}
satisfies (see
[
Nel11
, (7)]
)

(4.42)

{|a_{1}|^{2}}=c_{E}\frac{2\pi^{3}}{3}{\prod_{p|N^{\prime}}(1+\frac{1}{p})}\frac{(4\pi)^{k-1}}{\Gamma(k)L(1,\operatorname{Ad},\pi^{\prime})}

where
L(s,\operatorname{Ad},\pi^{\prime})
is the adjoint
L
-function. We have therefore

(4.43)

|a_{n}|^{2}=c_{E}\frac{2\pi^{3}}{3}\frac{\prod_{p|N^{\prime}}(1+1/p)}{L(1,\operatorname{Ad},\pi^{\prime})}\frac{(4\pi)^{k-1}n^{k-1}}{\Gamma(k)}|\lambda_{\pi^{\prime}}(n)|^{2}

Also, since
N^{\prime}
is squarefree
L(s,\operatorname{Ad},\pi^{\prime})
does not have a Landau-Siegel zero (see
[
HL94
]
) and one has

(4.44)

L(1,\operatorname{Ad},\pi^{\prime})=(kN^{\prime})^{o(1)}

so that

(4.45)

|a_{n}|^{2}\leq(kN^{\prime})^{o(1)}\frac{(4\pi)^{k-1}n^{k-1}}{\Gamma(k)}n^{o(1)}

5.
The spectral Side

Let
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
be a quadratic extension and let
W\subset V
be Hermitian spaces of dimensions
n
and
n+1
over
E
respectively. Set
G=U(V)
and
G^{\prime}=U(W)
be the corresponding unitary groups where
G^{\prime}
is embedded into
G
in the obvious way (as the stabilizer of
W^{\perp}\subset V
).

Let
(\pi,V_{\pi})
(resp.
(\pi^{\prime},V_{\pi^{\prime}})
) be cuspidal representations on
G(\mathbb{A})
(resp
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})
). Define the global Petersson pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
on
G({\mathbb{A}})
by

\displaystyle\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\rangle=\int_{G({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash G(\mathbb{A})}\varphi_{1}(g)\overline{\varphi_{2}(g)}dg,

where
dg
denote the Tamagawa measure on
G({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}).
Similarly one defines
\langle\varphi_{1}^{\prime},\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\rangle
on
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}}).

For any place
v
set
G_{v}=G({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
,
G^{\prime}_{v}=G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
and
dg_{v},\ dg^{\prime}_{v}
’s are local Haar measures on
G_{v},\ G^{\prime}_{v}
such that
\prod dg_{v}=dg,\ \prod dg^{\prime}_{v}=dg^{\prime}.

Under the decomposition
\pi=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
and
\pi^{\prime}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi^{\prime}_{v}
we fix a decomposition of either of the global inner products
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
into local ones as

\displaystyle\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle=\prod_{v}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{v}.

5.1.
Spectral Expansion

Given
k_{1},k_{2}
be integers such that
k_{2}\geq 2
,
k_{1}+k_{2}+2<0
and
k_{1}\equiv k_{2}\,(\mathrm{mod}\,{3})
, let

\Lambda=k_{1}\Lambda_{1}+k_{2}\Lambda_{2}

as in §
4.1
and let
D^{\Lambda}
be the corresponding holomorphic discrete series of
G(\mathbb{R})
.

Let

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}(N)=\{\pi=\pi_{\infty}\otimes\pi_{f}\in\mathcal{A}(G),\ \omega_{\pi}=\textbf{1},\ \pi_{\infty}\simeq D^{\Lambda},\ \pi_{f}^{K_{f}(N)}\not=\{0\}\}

the set of automorphic representations of
G({\mathbb{A}})
having trivial central character, whose archimedean component
\pi_{\infty}
is isomorphic to
D_{\Lambda}
and whose non-archimedean component
\pi_{f}
admits non-trivial
K_{f}(N)
-invariant vectors. The set
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}(N)
is finite and contains only cuspidal representations.

For any
\pi\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}(N)
, the subspace of lowest weight vectors of
\pi_{\infty}\simeq D_{\Lambda}
is one dimensional (generated by
\phi_{\circ}
say) and let
v_{\pi_{\infty}}\not=0
be such a non-zero vector. We define the finite dimensional vector spaces of automorphic forms

{\mathcal{V}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N):=\Im(\mathbb{C}v_{\pi_{\infty}}\otimes\pi_{f}^{K_{f}(N)}\hookrightarrow L^{2}([G]))

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{\Lambda}(N):=\bigoplus_{\pi\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}(N)}{\mathcal{V}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N).

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}
be the matrix

\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}=\prod_{p|N^{\prime}}\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{-1}&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix},

which we view as an element in
G(\mathbb{A})
(cf. §
4.2
); let

{\mathcal{V}}_{\pi,\Lambda}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(N):=\pi(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}).\mathcal{V}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)=\big\{\pi(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}})\varphi:\ \varphi\in\mathcal{V}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)\big\},

be their transforms under the action of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}
and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{\Lambda}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(N):=\bigoplus_{\pi\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}(N)}{\mathcal{V}}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N).

We fix orthonormal bases of the
{\mathcal{V}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)
and
{\mathcal{V}}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)
by

{\mathcal{B}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)\hbox{ and }{\mathcal{B}}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)=\pi(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}){\mathcal{B}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)

and finally let

{\mathcal{B}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)\hbox{ and }{\mathcal{B}}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}_{\Lambda}(N)

be their unions over the
\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}(N)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Notations be as above. Let
\ell\geq 1
be an integer whose prime divisors are all inert and corpime with
N
and let
f^{{\mathfrak{n}}}
be the function defined in §
4.4
, (
4.29
). The image of
R(f^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}})
is contained in
\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(N)
: let
\tilde{\varphi}
be an automorphic form on
G({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash G(\mathbb{A})
, then
R(f^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}})\varphi=0
unless
\tilde{\varphi}\in\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(N).
Moreover, for
\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}(N)
and
\tilde{\varphi}\in\Im(\mathbb{C}v_{\pi_{\infty}}\otimes\pi_{f}^{K_{f}(N)}\hookrightarrow L^{2}([G])),
we have

(5.1)

R(f^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}})\varphi=\frac{1}{d_{\Lambda}}\lambda_{\pi}(f_{\ell}).\varphi,

where
d_{\Lambda}
is the formal degree of
D^{\Lambda}
and

\lambda_{\pi}(f_{\ell})=\prod_{p|\ell}\lambda_{\pi_{p}}(f_{p})\in{\mathbb{C}}

is a scalar depending on
\pi_{\ell}=\otimes_{p|\ell}\pi_{p}
and on the test functions
(f_{p})_{p|\ell}
.

Proof.

Denote by
R^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(f)=\pi(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}})^{-1}R(f^{{\mathfrak{n}}})\pi(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}).
Then

\displaystyle R^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(f)\varphi(x)=

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{G}(\mathbb{A})}f(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}^{-1}y\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}})\varphi(x\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}^{-1}y\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}})dy=\int_{\overline{G}(\mathbb{A})}f(y)\varphi(xy)dy=\pi(f)\varphi(x).

From the definition of
f
:

f=f_{\infty}\otimes\bigotimes_{p}f_{p}\in C^{\infty}(G(\mathbb{A})),

where
f_{\infty}
is a matrix coefficient of
D^{\Lambda}
and each
f_{p}
is right
K_{p}(N)
-invariant, we see that the image of
R^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(f)
is contained in
\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}(N)
(in particular
R^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(f)
is zero on the Eisenstein or one the cuspidal spectrum: this comes from the choice of
f_{\infty}
to be the matrix coefficient of the holomorphic discrete series
D_{\Lambda}
). For the same reason we have

\pi_{\infty}(f_{\infty}).v_{\pi_{\infty}}=\frac{1}{d_{\Lambda}}v_{\pi_{\infty}}.

Moreover, for
p\mid\ell
,
\pi_{p}
is unramified and
\pi_{p}^{K_{p}(N)}
is one dimensional; since
f_{p}
is bi-
K_{p}(N)
invariant, for any
v_{p}\in\pi_{p}^{K_{p}(N)}
, one has

\pi_{p}(f_{p}).v_{p}=\lambda_{\pi_{p}}(f_{p}).v_{p}.

It follows that
{\mathcal{V}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)
is one dimensional made of factorable vectors and that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}(N)
one has

R(f)\varphi=\frac{1}{d_{\Lambda}}\prod_{p|\ell}\lambda_{\pi_{p}}(f_{p})\varphi=\frac{1}{d_{\Lambda}}\lambda_{\pi}(f_{\ell})\varphi

and that

R(f^{{\mathfrak{n}}})\pi(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}})\varphi=\frac{1}{d_{\Lambda}}\lambda_{\pi}(f_{\ell})\pi(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}})\varphi,

proving (
5.1
).
∎

By the spectral decomposition of the automorphic kernel and the above lemma, one has

(5.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in{G}(\mathbb{Q})}f^{{\mathfrak{n}}}(x^{-1}\gamma y)

Applying the expansion (
5.2
) into
J(f^{{\mathfrak{n}}})
(see (
3.3
)) we then conclude that

(5.3)

\displaystyle J(f^{{\mathfrak{n}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{{\Lambda}}}\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}(N)}\lambda_{\pi}(f_{\ell})\sum_{\tilde{\varphi}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})\overline{{\mathcal{P}}(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})}}{\langle\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{{\Lambda}}}\sum_{\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}(N)}\lambda_{\pi}(f_{\ell})\sum_{\tilde{\varphi}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)}\frac{\big|{\mathcal{P}}(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})\big|^{2}}{\langle\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{\varphi}\rangle}.

where
{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})
denote
the automorphic period integral

(5.4)

{\mathcal{P}}(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})=\int_{G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})}\tilde{\varphi}(g)\varphi^{\prime}(g)dg.

Since
\tilde{\varphi}
and
\varphi^{\prime}
are cusp forms,
{\mathcal{P}}(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})
converges absolutely and since
\mathcal{R}_{\Lambda}^{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{n}}}}(N)
is finite dimensional, the right hand side of (
5.3
) is absolutely converging.

Setting

{\mathcal{B}}_{\Lambda}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}}(N)=\bigsqcup_{\pi\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}(N)}{\mathcal{B}}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)

and for
\tilde{\varphi}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\pi,\Lambda}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}}(N)

\lambda_{\tilde{\varphi}}(f_{\ell})=\lambda_{\pi}(f_{\ell}),

(
5.3
) becomes

Lemma 5.2
.

Let notations be as above. We have

(5.5)

J(f^{{\mathfrak{n}}})=\frac{1}{d_{{\Lambda}}}\sum_{\tilde{\varphi}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\Lambda}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}}(N)}\lambda_{\tilde{\varphi}}(f_{\ell})\frac{\big|{\mathcal{P}}(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})\big|^{2}}{\langle\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{\varphi}\rangle}.

5.2.
The Ichino-Ikeda Conjecture for Unitary Groups

In this section we review the global Ichino-Ikeda conjecture for automorphic forms of
G\times G^{\prime}=U(V)\times U(W)
(e.g., see
[
Har14
]
, Conjecture 1.3), which is a refinement of the Gan-Gross-Prasad conjecture
[
GGP12
]
by giving an explicit formula relating the periods and central values of Rankin-Selberg
L
-functions. We now recall the definition of the local analogs of the global period integral (
5.4
) (see the beginning of §
5
for the notations)

We recall that
\pi\simeq\otimes_{v}\pi_{v}
and
\pi^{\prime}\simeq\otimes_{v}\pi^{\prime}_{v}
denote suitable automorphic representations of
U(V)
and
U(W)
which we assume are everywhere tempered. Their respective base changes to
\GL_{3,E}
and
\GL_{2,E}
are noted
\pi_{E}\simeq\otimes_{v}\pi_{E_{v}}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{E}\simeq\otimes_{v}\pi^{\prime}_{E_{v}}
. We denote by

L(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\prod_{p}L_{p}(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|p}\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}}L_{\mathfrak{p}}(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

the finite part of their Rankin-Selberg
L
-function and

\Lambda(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=L_{\infty}(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})L(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

its completed version (see Prop.
5.8
for the exact expresion of
L_{\infty}(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
). As recalled in the introduction, it admits analytic continuation to
{\mathbb{C}}
and a functional equation

(5.6)

\Lambda(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\varepsilon(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})C_{f}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})^{s}\Lambda(1-s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

In Proposition
5.9
) below we prove that

C_{f}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=N^{4}{N^{\prime}}^{6}

and that the root number equals

\varepsilon(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=+1.

Let

\Delta_{G}=\Lambda(M_{G}^{\vee}(1),0)

be the special (complete)
L
-value where
M_{G}^{\vee}(1)
is the twisted dual of the motive
M_{G}
associated to
G
by Gross
[
Gro97
]
. Locally, for
v
any place, we set

\Delta_{G,v}=L_{v}(M_{G}^{\vee}(1),0).

Explicitely, let
\eta=\prod_{v}\eta_{v}
denote the quadratic character of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}
associated to
E/\mathbb{Q}
by class field theory. We have

\displaystyle\Delta_{G,v}=\prod_{j=1}^{3}L_{v}(j,\eta^{j})=L_{v}(1,\eta)L_{v}(2,\textbf{1})L_{v}(3,\eta^{3}),

and

\Delta_{G}=\Lambda(1,\eta)\Lambda(2,\textbf{1})\Lambda(3,\eta^{3}).

We set

(5.7)

\Lambda(\pi,\pi^{\prime}):=\Delta_{G}\frac{\Lambda(1/2,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})}{\Lambda(1,\operatorname{Ad},\pi_{E})\Lambda(1,\operatorname{Ad},\pi_{E}^{\prime})}

and for any place
v
we set

(5.8)

L_{v}(\pi_{v},\pi^{\prime}_{v}):=\Delta_{G,v}\frac{L_{v}(1/2,\pi_{E_{v}}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E_{v}})}{L_{v}(1,\operatorname{Ad},\pi_{E_{v}})L_{v}(1,\operatorname{Ad},\pi^{\prime})}.

Note that, by temperedness, we have for any prime
p

L_{p}(\pi_{p},\pi^{\prime}_{p})=1+O(p^{-1/2})

where the implicit constant is absolute; moreover
there exists an absolute constant
C\geq 1
such that we have for any prime
p

(5.9)

C^{-1}\leq L_{p}(\pi_{p},\pi^{\prime}_{p})\leq C.

We also denote by

(5.10)

L(\pi,\pi^{\prime}):=\frac{\Lambda(\pi,\pi^{\prime})}{L_{\infty}(\pi_{\infty},\pi^{\prime}_{\infty})}

the "finite part" of the complete Euler product
\Lambda(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
.

Given any place
v
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and any tuple of local vectors

(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v},\xi^{\prime}_{1,v},\xi^{\prime}_{2,v})\in\pi_{v}\times\pi_{v}\times\pi^{\prime}_{v}\times\pi^{\prime}_{v},

the local period is defined formally by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{v}(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v};\xi_{1,v}^{\prime},\xi_{2,v}^{\prime}):=\int_{G^{\prime}_{v}}\langle\pi_{v}(g_{v})\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v}\rangle_{v}\cdot\overline{\langle\pi^{\prime}_{v}(g_{v})\xi^{\prime}_{1,v},\xi^{\prime}_{2,v}\rangle_{v}}dg_{v};

by a result of Harris
[
Har14
]
the integral
{\mathcal{P}}_{v}(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v};\xi_{1,v}^{\prime},\xi_{2,v}^{\prime})
converges absolutely when both
\pi_{v}
and
\pi_{v}^{\prime}
are tempered and

(5.11)

{\mathcal{P}}_{v}(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{1,v};\xi_{1,v}^{\prime},\xi_{1,v}^{\prime})\geq 0.

One then defines the unitarily and arithmetically normalized local period as

(5.12)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{*}(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v};\xi_{1,v}^{\prime},\xi_{2,v}^{\prime}):=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{v}(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v};\xi_{1,v}^{\prime},\xi_{2,v}^{\prime})}{\langle\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v}\rangle_{v}\langle\xi^{\prime}_{1,v},\xi_{2,v}^{\prime}\rangle_{v}},

(5.13)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{\natural}(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v};\xi_{1,v}^{\prime},\xi_{2,v}^{\prime}):=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{*}(\xi_{1,v},\xi_{2,v};\xi_{1,v}^{\prime},\xi_{2,v}^{\prime})}{L_{v}(\pi_{v},\pi^{\prime}_{v})}.

According to Theorem 2.12 in
[
Har14
]
, we have

{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{\natural}=1

for almost all places and

\displaystyle\prod_{v}{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{\natural}:\ (V_{\pi}\boxtimes{V}_{\pi})\otimes(V_{\pi^{\prime}}\boxtimes{V}_{\pi^{\prime}})\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}.

is a well defined
G(\mathbb{A})\times G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})
-invariant functional.

The global Ichino-Ikeda conjecture for the unitary groups
G\times G^{\prime}
then provides an explicit constant of proportionality between
|{\mathcal{P}}|^{2}
and
\prod{\mathcal{P}}^{\natural}_{v}.
It is now a theorem due to the recent work
[
BPLZZ21
]
:

Theorem 5.3

(
[
BPLZZ21
]
, Theorem 1.9)
.

Let notation be as before. Assume
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
are tempered. Let
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in V_{\pi},

\varphi_{1}^{\prime},\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\in V_{\pi^{\prime}}
be factorable vectors. We have

(5.14)

\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{1}^{\prime})\overline{{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi_{2},\varphi_{2}^{\prime})}}{\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\rangle\langle\varphi_{1}^{\prime},\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\rangle}=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Lambda(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\cdot\prod_{v}{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{\natural},

where
{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{\natural}
’s are defined by (
5.13
).

5.2.1.
Explicitation of the Ichino-Ikeda formula

In the next subsection, we explicitate the right-hand side of the formula (
5.14
) for the pairs

(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{1}^{\prime})=(\varphi_{2},\varphi_{2}^{\prime})=(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})\in\pi\times\pi^{\prime}

for
\tilde{\varphi}=\pi(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}})\varphi
appearing in (
5.5
) and
\varphi^{\prime}
discussed in §
4
.

Let us recall that

–

the representation
\pi=\otimes_{v\leq\infty}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
is a cuspidal representation of
G(\mathbb{A})
with trivial central character, of level
N
equal to
1
or to a prime inert in
E
such that
\pi_{\infty}\simeq D^{\Lambda},
with

\Lambda=k_{1}\Lambda_{1}+k_{2}\Lambda_{2},\ k_{1}+k_{2}+2<0,\ k_{2}\geq 0

and
k_{1}\equiv k_{2}\,(\mathrm{mod}\,{3})
namely,
D^{\Lambda}
is a holomorphic discrete series.

–

the representation
\pi^{\prime}=\otimes_{v\leq\infty}^{\prime}\pi^{\prime}_{v}
is a cuspidal representation of
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
with trivial central character, which at finite places is everywhere unramified excepted for at most one place
N^{\prime}
, split in
E
, where it is Steinberg and at the finite place has restriction, under the isomorphism
SU(W)\simeq\SL_{2}
, such that

\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}\simeq\pi^{\prime}_{k}

the holomorphic discrete series representation of even weight
k\geq 2
.

Let us now recall (this is a slight generalization of the discussion in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{sectempered}
) why the representations
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
are tempered. For
\pi^{\prime}
this is classical and due to Deligne (
[
Del71
]
). For
\pi
, if
N
is a prime (in which case
\pi_{N}
is the Steinberg representation whose parameters are
3
-dimensional and indecomposable),
\pi
cannot be globally endoscopic. If
\pi
has prime level
1
it could a priori be endoscopic but then come from a representation
\tau=\tau_{1}\otimes\tau_{2}
on
U(1,1)\times U(1)
as the parameter of
\tau_{\infty}
but match the parameter of
\pi_{\infty}
,
\tau_{1,\infty}
must in the discrete series and hence
\tau_{1}
must be tempered by Deligne and therefore
\tau
is tempered.

By the works of Kottwitz, Milne, Rogawski et al, as assembled in
[
LR92
]
(see Theorems A p. 291 and B p. 293) the
L
-function
L(s-1,\pi_{E})
is a factor of
L^{(2)}(s,\widetilde{S}^{K},V_{\ell})
, the
L
-function defined by the
\Gal(\overline{E}/E)
-action on
\mathrm{IH}^{2}_{et}(\overline{S}^{K}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}},V_{\ell})
, the intersection cohomology in degree
2
of the Baily-Borel-Satake compactification
\overline{S}^{K}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}
with coefficient in a local system
V
depending on the weight of
\pi_{\infty}
, of the associated Picard modular surface
S^{K}
for
K=K_{0}(N)
. By the work of Gabber, one knows that the intersection etale cohomology is pure and by the Beilinson-Bernstein-Deligne decomposition theorem,
\mathrm{IH}^{*}_{et}(\overline{S}^{K}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}},V_{\ell})
is a direct summand of
{H}^{*}_{et}(\widetilde{S}^{K}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}},V_{\ell})
for any smooth toroidal compactification
\widetilde{S}^{K}
of
S^{K}
relative to
\widetilde{S}^{K}\mapsto\overline{S}^{K}
. Now by Deligne’s proof of the Weil conjectures
[
Del74
]
, the eigenvalues of
\Frob_{\mathfrak{p}}
at any prime
{\mathfrak{p}}|p\nmid ND
acting on
{H}^{*}_{et}(\widetilde{S}^{K}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
have absolute value
\mathrm{Nr}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{p}})^{j/2}
(for
j
depending on the degree and
V_{\ell}
) from which it follows that
\pi_{E,p}
is tempered.

Remark 5.1
.

If
\pi
weren’t stable, it could be that only a factor of
L(s-1,\pi_{E})
divide
L^{(2)}(s,\widetilde{S}^{K},V_{\ell})
.

Let us recall that the automorphic forms
\varphi,\tilde{\varphi}=\pi(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}})\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
are factorable vectors and correspond to pure tensors which we denote by

(5.15)

\varphi\simeq{\otimes}^{\prime}_{v}\xi_{v},\ \tilde{\varphi}\simeq{\otimes}^{\prime}_{v}\tilde{\xi}_{v},\ \varphi^{\prime}\simeq{\otimes}^{\prime}_{v}\xi^{\prime}_{v}.

and that the local vectors

\xi_{v},\ \tilde{\xi}_{v}=\pi_{v}(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{v})\xi_{v},\ \xi^{\prime}_{v}

have the following properties and are uniquely defined up to scalars:

–

\tilde{\xi}_{v}=\xi_{v}
unless
v=p=N^{\prime}
.

–

If
v=\infty
,
\tilde{\xi}_{\infty}=\xi_{\infty}
is an highest weight vector of the minimal
K
-type of
D^{\Lambda}
and
\xi^{\prime}_{\infty}
is of minimal weight
k
(see §
4.5.1
).

–

For every
p
,
\xi_{p}
is
K_{p}(N)
-invariant and
\xi^{\prime}_{p}
is
K^{\prime}_{p}(N^{\prime})
-invariant.

–

In particular if
p
does not divide
D_{E}NN^{\prime}
, then
\xi_{p}=\tilde{\xi}_{p}
and
\xi^{\prime}_{p}
are invariant under the maximal compact subgroups
G({\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
and
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
respectively and
\pi_{p}
,
\pi^{\prime}_{p}
are unramified principal series representations.

Since
\pi^{\prime}
and
\pi
are everywhere tempered, formula (
5.14
) holds and in the next subsections we will evaluate the local period integrals and will provide for

\Lambda=k_{1}\Lambda_{1}+k_{2}\Lambda_{2},\ \ k_{1}=-k,\ \ k_{2}=-k/2

an explicit approximation of the central value

L(1/2,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
in terms of the square of the period
|{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})|^{2}
. Our main objective in this section is the following

Proposition 5.4
.

Let notations and assumptions be as in §
1.1.1
and as above. We have

(5.16)

\frac{\big|{\mathcal{P}}(\tilde{\varphi},\varphi^{\prime})\big|^{2}}{\langle\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{\varphi}\rangle\langle\varphi^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime}\rangle}\asymp\frac{1}{d_{k}N{N^{\prime}}^{2}}\frac{L(1/2,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})}{L(1,\pi_{E},\operatorname{Ad})L(1,\pi_{E}^{\prime},\operatorname{Ad})}

where
L(\cdot)
refers to the finite part of the
L
-functions, and the implicit (positive) constants in
\asymp
depends at most on the absolute discriminant of
E
.

Remark 5.2
.

In particular, this implies

(5.17)

L(1/2,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})\geq 0

whenever the local periods
{\mathcal{P}}_{v}^{\natural}
are non-zero.
This follows immediately from Theorem
5.14
and (
5.11
) and was likely known to experts. However, what we achieve here is an effective dependency between the sizes of the global period and the central
L
-value for our explicit test vectors. This will crucial for our forthcoming argument (see the proof of Theorem
1.1
in §
13.1
).

The proof is a consequence of Theorem
5.3
and of the following proposition which evaluate the local period (
5.13
) for each place
v
:

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
be the automorphic representations of
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
described in §
5.2.1
with
\Lambda=-k\Lambda_{1}+\frac{k}{2}\Lambda_{2}
,
and let
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}
be the factorable automorphic forms described in (
5.15
) and below. Let
v
be a place of
{\mathbb{Q}}
. We have

– Archimedean case: If
v=\infty
we have

(5.18)

L_{\infty}(\pi_{\infty},\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}){\mathcal{P}}_{\infty}^{\natural}(\xi_{\infty},\xi_{\infty};\xi_{\infty}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{\infty})=\frac{1}{d_{k}}

where

(5.19)

d_{k}={k-1}

is the formal degree of
\pi^{+}_{k}
.

– Unramified case: If
v=p
does not divide
2D_{E}NN^{\prime}
, one has

(5.20)

{\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})=1.

– The case
v=p|D_{E}
: we have

(5.21)

\displaystyle\Big|L_{p}(\pi_{p},\pi^{\prime}_{p}){\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})-1\Big|\leq\frac{71}{18}\frac{1}{p^{2}}.

In particular, we have

(5.22)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\leq{\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})\leq C,

for some absolute constant
C
.

– The case
v=p=N
: we have

(5.23)

L_{p}(\pi_{p},\pi^{\prime}_{p}){\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})=\frac{1}{p}(1-\frac{1}{p})+\frac{O}{p^{2}}

with
|O|\leq 4
. In particular (since
p\geq 3
) we have

(5.24)

\frac{C^{-1}}{p}\leq{\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})\leq\frac{C}{p}

for some absolute constant
C\geq 1
.

– The case
v=p=N^{\prime}
: we have

(5.25)

L_{p}(\pi_{p},\pi^{\prime}_{p}){\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\tilde{\xi}_{p},\tilde{\xi}_{p};\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p})=\frac{1}{p^{2}-1}(1+\frac{O}{p})

with

|O|\leq 10^{6}
. In particular for
p>10^{6}
we have

(5.26)

\frac{C^{-1}}{p^{2}}\leq{\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}({\tilde{\xi}}_{p},{\tilde{\xi}}_{p};\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p})\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}}

for some absolute constant
C\geq 1
.

5.3.
The archimedean local period

In this subsection we discuss (
5.18
). We start with the following proposition which justifies our choice
(k_{1},k_{2})
:

Proposition 5.6
.

Let notations and assumption be as above. The global period (
5.4
) vanishes unless
k_{1}=-k
and
k_{2}=k/2
.

Proof.

For
\theta\in[-\pi,\pi),
let

z^{\prime}(\theta)=\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta},1,e^{i\theta})\in Z_{G^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}),\ \kappa(\theta)=\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta},1,e^{-i\theta})\in G^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}),

\widetilde{z}(\theta)=\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta/3},e^{-2i\theta/3},e^{i\theta/3})\in SU(V)(\mathbb{R}).

Then an explicit computation (as in the proof of Proposition
4.5
) shows that for

\forall g\in G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}),\ \varphi(g\widetilde{z}(\theta))=e^{-(k_{1}+2k_{2})i\theta}\varphi(g).

One has for any
\theta

(5.27)

\varphi^{\prime}(gz^{\prime}(\theta))=\varphi^{\prime}(g),\ \varphi^{\prime}(g\kappa(\theta))=e^{-ik\theta}\varphi^{\prime}(g).

The first equality implies that

\varphi(gz^{\prime}(\theta))=\varphi(g)

(or the period integral would be
0
). Since

z^{\prime}(\theta)\tilde{z}(\theta)^{-1}=\operatorname{diag}(e^{2i\theta/3},e^{2i\theta/3},e^{2i\theta/3})\in Z_{G}({\mathbb{R}})

and
\varphi
is invariant under the center one has

\varphi(g)=\varphi(gz^{\prime}(\theta))=\varphi(g\tilde{z}(\theta))=e^{-(k_{1}+2k_{2})i\theta}\varphi(g)

and

k_{1}+2k_{2}=0.

The computation in Lemma
9.6
shows that

\varphi(g\kappa(\theta))=e^{ik_{1}\theta}\varphi(g)

and by the second equality in (
5.27
) we have
k+k_{1}=0
or otherwise
{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})=0
.

∎

Remark 5.3
.

One could also obtain Lemma
5.6
through the relative trace formula by computing the geometric side, i.e., orbital integrals: as a consequence of Lemma
4.7
we have
f_{\infty}(gz(\theta))=e^{-(k_{1}+2k_{2})i\theta}f_{\infty}(g),
for all
g\in G^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}).
Hence, if
k_{1}+2k_{2}\neq 0,
the geometric side vanishes. Then the sum of
|{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})|^{2}
is zero. Since each term is nonnegative, then each periods is vanishing.

Remark 5.4
.

Due to Lemma
5.6
, we will take from now on
(k_{1},k_{2})=(-k,k/2)
, i.e.

(5.28)

\Lambda=-k\Lambda_{1}+\frac{k}{2}\Lambda_{2}.

To insure absolute convergence of various integrals later we will moreover assume that

k\geq 32

an even integer. In the sequel, to simplify notations and since
\Lambda
is defined in terms of
k
, we will sometimes replace the indice
\Lambda
by
k
and write
{\mathcal{V}}_{k}(N)
for
{\mathcal{V}}_{\Lambda}(N)
,
{\mathcal{B}}_{\pi,k}(N)
for
{\mathcal{B}}_{\pi,\Lambda}(N)
, etc…

The next lemma provides the value of
d_{\Lambda}
:

Lemma 5.7
.

Let

\Lambda=k_{1}\Lambda_{1}+k_{2}\Lambda_{2}=-k\Lambda_{1}+\frac{k}{2}\Lambda_{2}

defined in (
5.28
) and
D^{\Lambda}
be the corresponding holomorphic discrete series. When
dg
is the Euler-Poincaré measure, its formal degree equals

(5.29)

d_{\Lambda}=\frac{(k_{1}+1)(k_{2}+1)(k_{1}+k_{2}+2)}{6}=\frac{(2k-2)(k+2)(k-6)}{3}\asymp\frac{2}{3}k^{3}.

Proof.

We recall that the simple positive root in this case are

e_{1}-e_{2}
and
e_{2}-e_{3}
where
e_{i}
are the standard basis vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and the root space is the hyperplane
\{(x,y,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3},x+y+z=0\}
. The
\Lambda_{j},\ j=1,2
are given by

\Lambda_{1}=\frac{1}{3}(2,1,-1),\ \Lambda_{2}=\frac{1}{3}(1,1,-2).

Let
\rho
be half the sum of the positive roots, it is given by

\rho=\frac{1}{2}(e_{1}-e_{2}+e_{2}-e_{3}+e_{1}-e_{3})=e_{1}-e_{3}=(1,0,-1).

Consider the Weyl reflections

S_{1}:(x,y,z)\mapsto(y,x,z),\ S_{2}:(x,y,z)\mapsto(x,z,y)

and let
\Lambda^{\prime}
be such that

k_{1}\Lambda_{1}+k_{2}\Lambda_{2}=S_{1}\circ S_{2}(\Lambda^{\prime}+\rho)-\rho.

Remark 5.5
.

In
[
Wal76
, Lemma 9.4]

\langle\lambda+\rho,\alpha\rangle/\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle
should be
\langle\lambda+\rho,\alpha\rangle/\langle\rho,\alpha\rangle
.

Let us compute the Langlands parameter
\Lambda^{\prime}=(a,b,c)
:

S_{1}\circ S_{2}(\Lambda^{\prime}+\rho)-\rho=(c-2,a+1,b+1)=\frac{k_{1}}{3}(2,-1,-1)+\frac{k_{2}}{3}(1,1,-2)

so that

(5.30)

\Lambda^{\prime}=(\frac{-k_{1}+k_{2}}{3}-1,-\frac{k_{1}+2k_{2}}{3}-1,\frac{2k_{1}+k_{2}}{3}+2).

We have the Blattner parameter

\Lambda^{\prime}+\rho=(\frac{-k_{1}+k_{2}}{3},-\frac{k_{1}+2k_{2}}{3}-1,\frac{2k_{1}+k_{2}}{3}+1)

and

\langle\Lambda^{\prime}+\rho,\alpha\rangle=\begin{cases}k_{2}+1&\ \alpha=e_{1}-e_{2}\\
-k_{1}-k_{2}-2&\ \alpha=e_{2}-e_{3}\\
-k_{1}-1&\ \alpha=e_{1}-e_{3}.\end{cases}

Then (
5.29
) follows from Harish-Chandra’s formula
[
HC66
]
that

\displaystyle d(D^{\Lambda})=\frac{1}{3}\prod_{\alpha>0}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{\prime}+\rho,\alpha\rangle}{\langle\rho,\alpha\rangle},

with the product over all the positive roots
\alpha\in\{e_{1}-e_{2},e_{2}-e_{3},e_{1}-e_{3}\}.

∎

We can now evaluate explicitly the archimedean local period integral:

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
be the automorphic representations of
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
described in §
5.2.1
.
We have (with
k_{1}=-k,\ k_{2}=k/2
)

(5.31)

L_{\infty}(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}\left(s+\frac{3}{2}\right)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}\left(s+\frac{k}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\\
\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}\left(s+\frac{k}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}\left(s+k-\frac{5}{2}\right)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}\left(s+{k}-\frac{3}{2}\right).

where

\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s)=2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s),\ \Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s)=\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2).

Proof.

Recall that the Langlands parameter of the base changed representation of the holomorphic discrete series of weight
k
is
(\frac{k-1}{2},-\frac{k-1}{2}),
i.e.,

z\mapsto\operatorname{diag}((\overline{z}/z)^{\frac{k-1}{2}},(\overline{z}/z)^{-\frac{k-1}{2}}).

Therefore, the archimedean parameter of the base changed representation
\pi^{\prime}_{E}
is given by

\displaystyle(\frac{k-1}{2},\frac{1-k}{2}):\ z\mapsto\operatorname{diag}((\bar{z}/z)^{\frac{k-1}{2}},(\bar{z}/z)^{-\frac{k-1}{2}}),

Recall that (cf. (
5.30
)) the Langlands parameter of
\pi_{\infty}
is

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime}=(\frac{-k_{1}+k_{2}}{3}-1,-\frac{k_{1}+2k_{2}}{3}-1,\frac{2k_{1}+k_{2}}{3}+2)=(\frac{k}{2}-1,-1,-\frac{k}{2}+2).

Let
\pi_{\infty,\mathbb{C}}
be the base change of
\pi_{\infty}
to
\mathrm{GL}_{3}(\mathbb{C}).
Then
the archimedean parameter of
\pi_{\infty,\mathbb{C}}\otimes\pi_{\infty,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}
is given by

(\frac{k}{2}-1,-1,-\frac{k}{2}+2)\otimes(\frac{k-1}{2},\frac{1-k}{2})=(k-\frac{3}{2},\frac{k}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2},-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{1}{2},-k+\frac{5}{2}).

and the result follows since for
r\in\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{Z}}

L_{\infty}(z\mapsto(\overline{z}/z)^{r},s)=L_{\infty}((z\overline{z})^{s}(\overline{z}/z)^{r})=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+|r|)

∎

5.3.1.
Proof of (
5.18
)

By definition we have

{\mathcal{P}}_{\infty}(\xi_{\infty},\xi_{\infty};\xi_{\infty}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{\infty})=\int_{G^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}})}\langle g_{\infty}.\xi_{\infty},\xi_{\infty}\rangle_{\infty}\overline{\langle\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}(g_{\infty})\xi^{\prime}_{\infty},\xi^{\prime}_{\infty}\rangle}_{\infty}dg_{\infty}.

Note that by Lemma
4.7
,

\frac{\langle\pi_{\infty}(g_{\infty})\xi_{\infty},\xi_{\infty}\rangle_{\infty}}{\langle\xi_{\infty},\xi_{\infty}\rangle_{\infty}}=f_{\infty}(g_{\infty}).

Then by Lemma
4.8
and the Schur orthogonality relations we obtain

\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{\infty}(\xi_{\infty},\xi_{\infty};\xi_{\infty}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{\infty})}{\langle\xi_{\infty},\xi_{\infty}\rangle_{\infty}\langle\xi^{\prime}_{\infty},\xi^{\prime}_{\infty}\rangle_{\infty}}=\int_{{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})}f_{\infty}(g_{\infty})\frac{\langle\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}(g_{\infty})\xi^{\prime}_{\infty},\xi^{\prime}_{\infty}\rangle_{\infty}}{\langle\xi^{\prime}_{\infty},\xi^{\prime}_{\infty}\rangle_{\infty}}dg_{\infty}=\frac{1}{d_{\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}}}.

where
d_{\pi^{\prime}_{\infty}}=d_{k}
is the formal degree of
\pi^{+}_{k}

Then (
5.18
) follows.
∎

5.4.
The root number and the conductor of
L(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

In this section we compute the root number and the arithmetic conductor of
L(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
. First we observe that
\pi_{E}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{E}
are conjugate self-dual, ie. if
c\in\Aut_{\mathbb{Q}}(E)
denote the non-trivial automorphism, we have

\pi_{E}\circ c\simeq\pi^{\vee}_{E}\simeq\overline{\pi}_{E},\ {\pi^{\prime}}_{E}\circ c\simeq{\pi^{\prime}}^{\vee}_{E}\simeq\overline{\pi^{\prime}}_{E}

( and since the representations are unitary
\pi^{\vee}_{E}\simeq\overline{\pi}_{E},\ {\pi^{\prime}}^{\vee}_{E}\simeq\overline{\pi^{\prime}}_{E}
). In particular, the functional equation indeed relates
\Lambda(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
to
\Lambda(1-s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
and
\varepsilon(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})\in\{\pm 1\}
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
be the automorphic representations of
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
described in §
5.2.1
; let
\pi_{E}
,
\pi^{\prime}_{E}
be the corresponding base change to
\GL(3,{\mathbb{A}}_{E})
and
\GL(2,{\mathbb{A}}_{E})
and
L(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
be (the finite part of) its associated Rankin-Selberg
L
-function. Its arithmetic conductor equal

C_{f}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=N^{4}{N^{\prime}}^{6}

and its root number equals

\varepsilon(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=+1.

Consequently, its analytic conductor
C(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
satisfies (for
\Re s=1/2
)

(5.32)

C(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})\asymp N^{4}{N^{\prime}}^{6}|s|^{4}(|s|+k)^{8}.

In particular for
s=1/2
one has the convexity bound

L(1/2,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})\ll(kNN^{\prime})^{o(1)}C(1/2,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})^{1/4}\ll(kNN^{\prime})^{o(1)}k^{3}N{N^{\prime}}^{3/2}.

Proof.

Since
(N,N^{\prime})=1,
the arithmetic conductor of
L(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
is simply

C_{f}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=(N^{2})^{2}({N^{\prime}}^{2})^{3}=N^{4}{N^{\prime}}^{6};

indeed for
N
a prime inert in
E
, the conductor of the Steinberg representation for
\GL_{3}(E_{N})
is
N^{2}
(the norm of the ideal
N{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{N}}
) and for
N^{\prime}
a prime split in
E
,
\GL_{2}(E_{N^{\prime}})\simeq\GL_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{N^{\prime}})^{2}
and
\pi_{N^{\prime}}\simeq{\mathrm{St}}_{N^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathrm{St}}_{N^{\prime}}
has conductor
{N^{\prime}}^{2}
.

By (
5.31
) the archimedean conductor is (for
\Re s=1/2
),

\displaystyle C_{\infty}(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

\displaystyle\asymp|s+1/2|^{2}|s+3/2|^{2}|s+k/2-3/2|^{2}

\displaystyle\ \ \times|s+k/2+1/2|^{2}|s+k-5/2|^{2}|s+k-3/2|^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp|s|^{4}(|s|+k)^{8}.

and the analytic conductor

C(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=C_{\infty}(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})C_{f}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

satisfies (
5.32
).

The convexity bound follows from apply the approximate functional equation for
L(1/2,\pi_{E}\times\pi_{E}^{\prime})

and from the following bounds for the coefficients of
L(s,\pi_{E}\times\pi_{E}^{\prime})

\lambda_{\pi_{E}\times\pi_{E}^{\prime}}(n)\ll_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}

for any
\varepsilon>0
(the later is a consequence of the temperedness of
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
).

Let us turn to the computation of the root number: ita decomposes as a product of local root numbers (along the places of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and, say, relative to the usual unramified additive character)

\varepsilon(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\varepsilon_{\infty}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})\prod_{p}\varepsilon_{p}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

and for any such place
v
we have the further factorisation

\varepsilon_{v}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\prod_{w|v}\varepsilon_{w}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})

If
v=p\not|NN^{\prime}
then
\pi_{E}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{E}
are un ramified at any place
w|p
and

\varepsilon_{w}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=1.

If
p=N
which is inert in
E
, that
\pi^{\prime}_{E}
is unramified and

\varepsilon_{p}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\varepsilon_{p}(\pi_{E})^{2}=1

(
\pi_{E,p}=\mathrm{St}_{p}
and
\varepsilon_{p}(\pi_{E})=\pm 1
).

If
p|N^{\prime}
then

\varepsilon_{p}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{p}}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})\varepsilon_{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=(\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{p}}(\pi^{\prime}_{E})\varepsilon_{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\pi^{\prime}_{E}))^{3}

since
\pi_{E}
is unramified at these places. Also since
\pi^{\prime}_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is the base change of the Steinberg representation,
\pi^{\prime}_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}\simeq\pi^{\prime}_{E_{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
and

\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{p}}(\pi^{\prime}_{E})\varepsilon_{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\pi^{\prime}_{E})=1
.

Finally for
v=\infty
the unique archimedean place we have seen above that
\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E}
has parameters

(k-\frac{3}{2},\frac{k}{2}-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2},-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{1}{2},-k+\frac{5}{2}).

By
[
Tat79
, §3.2]
for
r\in\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{Z}}

\varepsilon_{\infty}(z\rightarrow(\overline{z}/z)^{r})=i^{2r}

and in this case we have

k-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{k}{2}-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{1}{2}-k+\frac{5}{2}=0

hence

\varepsilon_{\infty}(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})=1.

∎

Remark 5.6
.

Notice that by
[
GGP12
]
,
\pi_{E}
is conjugate orthogonal and
\pi^{\prime}_{E}
conjugate symplectic so with this information only the sign
\varepsilon(\pi_{E}\times\pi^{\prime}_{E})
coudl be
+1
or
-1
.

5.5.
The local periods at unramified primes

Let now
v=p
be a prime that does not divide
NN^{\prime}D_{E}
then (see the discussion in §
5.2.1
)
\xi_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}
are unramified vectors and the 7 conditions on page 308 of
[
Har14
]
(see also
[
II10
]
, (U1)-(U6), p.5) are satisfied for
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}.
Consequently, by Theorem 2.12 of
[
Har14
]
,

{\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})=1

which is (
5.20
).

5.6.
The local periods at primes ramified in
E/{\mathbb{Q}}

In this section we establish (
5.21
). Let
p=\mathfrak{p}^{2}
be a ramified prime and
\varpi
be a uniformizer of
\mathfrak{p}.
Let

A_{n}=\operatorname{diag}(\varpi^{n},1,\overline{\varpi}^{-n}),\ n\geq 0.

Since
\pi_{p}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{p}
are tempered principal series, we may assume
\pi_{p}=\operatorname{Ind}\chi_{p}
and
\pi_{p}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ind}\chi_{p}^{\prime},
for some unramified unitary characters
\chi_{p}
and
\chi_{p}^{\prime}
of the respective diagonal tori. Denote by
\gamma_{p}=\chi_{p}(A_{1})
and
\gamma_{p}^{\prime}=\chi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{1}^{\prime}).

By Macdonald’s formula (cf.
[
Mac71
]
or
[
Cas80
]
) we have for
n\geq 0,

(5.33)

\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{n})\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}=\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})\gamma_{p}^{\prime n}-(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-n-1}}{p^{n}\big[(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})-(1-p^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}_{p})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1}\big]},

(5.34)

\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(A_{n})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}}=\\
\frac{1}{p^{2n}}\Big[\frac{(\gamma_{p}-p^{-2})(1+p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{-1})\gamma_{p}^{n}-(\gamma_{p}^{-1}-p^{-2})(1+p^{-1}\gamma_{p})\gamma_{p}^{-n}}{(\gamma_{p}-p^{-2})(1+p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{-1})\gamma_{p}-(\gamma_{p}^{-1}-p^{-2})(1+p^{-1}\gamma_{p})\gamma_{p}^{-1}}\Big]

and these inner product vanish for
n<0
.

Therefore, if the Haar measure on
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is such that
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
has measure
1
, by the Cartan decomposition we have

(5.35)

\int\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(g_{p})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(g_{p})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}dg_{p}=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(A_{n})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(A_{n})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}},

where the integral on the left hand side is taken over
{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

A straightforward calculation shows that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{n})\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}\Bigg|=\Bigg|\frac{1-\gamma_{p}^{\prime 2n+1}-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime}(1-\gamma_{p}^{\prime 2n-1})}{(1+p^{-1})(1-\gamma_{p}^{\prime})p^{n}}\Bigg|.

Then expanding the fractions into geometric series and appealing to triangle inequality, we obtain, when
n\geq 1,
that

(5.36)

L_{n}^{\prime}:=\Bigg|\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{n})\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{2n+1+p^{-1}(2n-1)}{(1+p^{-1})p^{n}}.

Similarly, after a straightforward calculation we have

\displaystyle L_{n}:=\Bigg|\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(A_{n})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}}\Bigg|=\Bigg|\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{p}^{n+1}+p^{-1}(1-p^{-1})\widetilde{\gamma}_{p}^{n}-p^{-3}\widetilde{\gamma}_{p}^{n-1}}{p^{2n}(1+p^{-3})(\gamma_{p}-\gamma_{p}^{-1})}\Bigg|,

where

\widetilde{\gamma}_{p}^{m}:=\gamma_{p}^{m}-\gamma_{p}^{-m},\ m\in\mathbb{Z}.

Expanding the fractions into geometric series and appealing to triangle inequality we then obtain

(5.37)

L_{n}\leq\frac{2+(1-p^{-3})\big[2{\left\lfloor\frac{n-1}{2}\right\rfloor}+\frac{(-1)^{n}+1}{2}\big]+p^{-1}(1-p^{-1})\big[2{\left\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\right\rfloor}+\frac{(-1)^{n-1}+1}{2}\big]}{(1+p^{-3})p^{2n}}

Therefore, we have from (
5.36
) and (
5.37
) that

(5.38)

\Bigg|\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(A_{1})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(A_{1}^{\prime})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{3+p^{-1}}{p+1}\cdot\frac{2+p^{-1}(1-p^{-1})}{(1+p^{-3})p^{2}}<\frac{6-p^{-1}}{p^{3}+1};

and

(5.39)

\Bigg|\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(A_{2})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(A_{2}^{\prime})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{(5+3p^{-1})(3+2p^{-1})}{(1+p^{-1})p^{6}};

moreover, when
n\geq 3,

(5.40)

\Bigg|\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(A_{n})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(A_{n})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}\Bigg|<\frac{2n^{2}+5n+2}{p^{3n}}.

Combining (
5.38
), (
5.39
) with (
5.40
) we then conclude that

(5.41)

\sum_{n\geq 1}L_{n}^{\prime}L_{n}\leq\frac{6-p^{-1}}{p^{3}+1}+\frac{(5+3p^{-1})(3+2p^{-1})}{(1+p^{-1})p^{6}}+\sum_{n\geq 3}\frac{2n^{2}+5n+2}{p^{3n}}.

By induction one has
2n^{2}+5n+2\leq 5\cdot 2^{n}
for
n\geq 2
. Therefore, substituting this estimate into (
5.41
) and computing the geometric series we then obtain

(5.42)

\sum_{n\geq 1}\Bigg|\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(A_{1})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(A_{1}^{\prime})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}\Bigg|\leq H(p),

where the auxiliary arithmetic function
H(\cdot)
is defined by

\displaystyle H(n):=\frac{6-n^{-1}}{n^{3}+1}+\frac{(5+3n^{-1})(3+2n^{-1})}{(1+n^{-1})n^{6}}+\frac{40}{n^{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{n^{3}-2},\ n\geq 2.

For
n\geq 2,
one has
n^{2}H(n)\geq(n+1)^{2}H(n+1)
and
H(p)\leq\frac{4H(2)}{p^{2}}=\frac{71}{18p^{2}}.
Hence, combining (
5.35
) with (
5.42
) we then obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(g_{p})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(g_{p})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}dg_{p}-1\Bigg|\leq\frac{4H(2)}{p^{2}}=\frac{71}{18p^{2}}

and (
5.21
) follows.
∎

5.7.
The matrix coefficient of the Steinberg representation for
U(V)

We continue to assume that
p
is inert in
E
. Let
\mu=\mu_{p}
be a Haar measure on
G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
Denote by
W_{0}=\big\{\textbf{1}_{p},J\big\}.
For
n\geq 1,
set

W_{n}=\big\{A_{n},JA_{n},A_{n}J,JA_{n}J\big\}.

Let

W:=\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}W_{n}.

By Lemma
A.6
(see the Appendix) and the Cartan decomposition we then obtain

(5.43)

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})A_{n}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\bigsqcup_{w\in W}I_{p}wI_{p}.

By Lemma
A.4
one has
\mu(I_{p}JI_{p})=p^{3}\mu(I_{p}).
Moreover, by Lemma
A.5
, for
n\geq 1,

\displaystyle\mu(I_{p}A_{n}I_{p})=p^{4n}\mu(I_{p}),\ \mu(I_{p}JA_{n}I_{p})=p^{4n-3}\mu(I_{p}),

\displaystyle\ \mu(I_{p}A_{n}JI_{p})=p^{4n+3}\mu(I_{p}),\ \mu(I_{p}JA_{n}JI_{p})=p^{4n}\mu(I_{p}).

Then for
w\in W,
there exists a unique integer
\lambda(w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

\mu(I_{p}wI_{p})=p^{\lambda(w)}\mu(I_{p}).

In particular,
\lambda(\textbf{1}_{p})=0,

\lambda(J)=3;
and for
n\geq 1,

\lambda(A_{n})=4n,\ \lambda(JA_{n})=4n-3,\ \lambda(A_{n}J)=4n+3\hbox{ and }\lambda(JA_{n}J)=4n.

Thus, the Poincaré series

(5.44)

\sum_{w\in W}p^{-2\lambda(w)}=1+p^{-6}+(2+p^{6}+p^{-6})\cdot\sum_{n\geq 1}p^{-8n}

converges absolutely.

Let
\Xi_{p}
be the
I_{p}
-bi-invariant function on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
defined by

\Xi_{p}(w)=(-p)^{-\lambda(w)}\mu(I_{p}),\ w\in W.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
f
be a function on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
defined by

f(pk)=\delta_{P}(p)\Xi_{p}(k),

for
p\in P(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
k\in G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
\delta_{P}
the module character of the Borel subgroup
P.
Then

(5.45)

\varphi_{f}(g):=\int_{I_{p}}f(\kappa g)d\kappa=\Xi_{p}(g)\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p}),\quad\forall g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Proof.

Let
g=pk
be the Iwasawa decomposition, then by the Iwahori decomposition
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}JI_{p}
we obtain

\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}JI_{p}}f(g\kappa)d\kappa=\int_{G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}f(p\kappa)d\kappa\\
=\delta_{P}(p)\int_{G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\Xi(\kappa)d\kappa=\delta_{P}(p)(1+p^{3}\Xi_{p}(J))\mu(I_{p}),

where the last equality comes from Lemma
A.4
. Notice that
1+p^{3}\Xi_{p}(J)=0
, so that

(5.46)

\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}JI_{p}}f(g\kappa)d\kappa=0,\quad\forall g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Let
g=pk
with
k\in I_{p}.
Then we have by definition

\displaystyle\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p}}f(g\kappa)d\kappa=

\displaystyle\delta_{P}(p)\cdot\bigg[\int_{I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(\kappa)d\kappa+\int_{I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(\kappa)d\kappa\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{P}(p)\cdot\left(\mu(I_{p})+\Xi_{p}(JA_{1})\mu(I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p})\right)=0.

When
g=pk
and
k\in I_{p}JI_{p},
we can write
k=n(\delta,\tau)J\kappa_{1}
by (
A.11
). So

\displaystyle\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p}}f(g\kappa)d\kappa=

\displaystyle\int_{I_{p}}f(pn(\delta,\tau)J\kappa)d\kappa+\int_{I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p}}f(pn(\delta,\tau)J\kappa)d\kappa

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{P}(p)\cdot\bigg[\int_{I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(J\kappa)d\kappa+\int_{I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(J\kappa)d\kappa\bigg].

By Lemma
A.5
we have

\displaystyle\int_{I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(J\kappa)d\kappa+\int_{I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(J\kappa)d\kappa=

\displaystyle\Big[\Xi_{p}(J)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\in p\mathcal{O}_{p}/p^{2}\mathcal{O}_{p}\\
\tau+\overline{\tau}=0\end{subarray}}\Xi_{p}(n(0,\tau)A_{1})\Big]\mu(I_{p})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Xi_{p}(J)\mu(I_{p})+p\Xi_{p}(A_{1})\mu(I_{p}).

Note that
\Xi_{p}(J)+p\Xi_{p}(A_{1})=-p^{-3}+p\cdot(-p)^{-4}=0.
Hence, we have

(5.47)

\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}JA_{1}I_{p}}f(g\kappa)d\kappa=0,\quad\forall g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Note that the function
\varphi_{f}
is
I_{p}
-bi-invariant. So we only need to verify (
5.45
) for all
g\in W.
Clearly, (
5.45
) holds for
g=\textbf{1}_{p}\in W.
Moreover, by (
5.46
) and (
5.47
), (
5.45
) holds for
g\in\{J,JA_{1}\}\subset W.
Hence, we have

(5.48)

\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}wI_{p}}\varphi_{f}(g\kappa)d\kappa=0,\ \forall w\in\big\{J,JA_{1}\big\},\ g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Hence, expanding (
5.48
) we then see
\varphi_{f}(w)=\Xi_{p}(w)
holds for all
w\in W
with
\lambda(w)\leq 3.

Let
n\geq 3.
Suppose
\varphi_{f}(w)=\Xi_{p}(w)
holds for all
w\in W
such that
\lambda(w)\leq n.
Let
w^{\prime}\in W
be such that
\lambda(w^{\prime})=n+1.
Then there exists
w_{1}\in\big\{J,JA_{1}\big\},
and
w_{2}\in W-\big\{\textbf{1}_{p}\big\}
with
w_{2}w_{1}=w^{\prime}
and
\lambda(w_{2})+\lambda(w_{1})=n+1.
Explicitly, suppose
w^{\prime}\in W_{m},

m\geq 1.
When
w^{\prime}=A_{m}J,
then
w_{1}=J
and
w_{2}=A_{m};
when
w^{\prime}=JA_{m}J,
then
w_{1}=J
and
w_{2}=JA_{m};
when
w^{\prime}=A_{m},
then
w_{1}=JA_{1}
and
w_{2}=A_{m-1}J;
when
w^{\prime}=JA_{m},
then
w_{1}=JA_{1}
and
w_{2}=JA_{m-1}J.

We have
1\leq\lambda(w_{1})\leq 3
and
\lambda(w_{2})\leq n.
Also, by Lemma
A.5
one has

I_{p}w_{2}I_{p}w_{1}I_{p}=I_{p}wI_{p}.

Hence, by our assumption, and taking
g=w_{1},
one then has

(5.49)

\int_{I_{p}}\varphi_{f}(w_{2}\kappa)d\kappa+\int_{I_{p}w_{1}I_{p}}\varphi_{f}(w_{2}\kappa)d\kappa=\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}w_{1}I_{p}}\varphi_{f}(w_{2}\kappa)d\kappa=0.

Since
\varphi_{f}
is bi-invariant under
I_{p}
and
I_{p}w_{1}I_{p}w_{2}I_{p}=I_{p}wI_{p},
(
5.49
) then becomes

(5.50)

\varphi_{f}(w_{2})+\frac{\mu(I_{p}w_{1}I_{p})}{\mu(I_{p})}\varphi_{f}(w)=0,\ i.e.,\ \varphi_{f}(w_{2})+q^{\lambda(w_{1})}\varphi_{f}(w^{\prime})=0.

By assumption,
\varphi_{f}(w_{2})=\Xi_{p}(w_{2}).
Hence by (
5.50
),

\varphi_{f}(w^{\prime})=-p^{-\lambda(w_{1})}\Xi_{p}(w_{2})=(-p)^{-\lambda(w_{1})-\lambda(w_{2})}\mu(I_{p})=\Xi_{p}(w^{\prime}).

Thus (
5.45
) follows by induction.
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
p
be a prime inert in
E
and
\mathrm{St}_{p}
be the Steinberg representation of
G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
; the function
\Xi_{p}
is a matrix coefficient of
\mathrm{St}_{p}.
Precisely, let
\xi_{p}\neq 0
be a local new vector in
\mathrm{St}_{p}
(a generator of the one dimensional space of
I_{p}
-invariant vectors), then

(5.51)

\frac{\langle\mathrm{St}_{p}(g)\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}}=\frac{\Xi_{p}(g)}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})},\quad\forall\ g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}
be the vector space spanned by the right translates of the function
\Xi_{p}.
Then
{\mathcal{V}}
is a smooth representation of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which we denote by
(\pi_{p},{\mathcal{V}})
. Suppose that
\pi_{p}
is reducible: there exists some nonzero
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-invariant subspace
{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}\subsetneq{\mathcal{V}}
and some
g_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
\pi_{p}(g_{0})\Xi_{p}\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}
and

\Xi_{p}(g_{0})=\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p}\cdot g_{0})=\pi_{p}(g_{0})\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})\neq 0.

Let
g_{1}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).
Denote by

(5.52)

\varphi(g):=\int_{I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(g_{1}\kappa g)d\kappa.

Then
\varphi
is a function of
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
bi-invariant under
I_{p}.
Moreover, similar computation as in (
5.46
) and (
5.47
) shows that

(5.53)

\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}wI_{p}}\Xi_{p}(g^{\prime}\kappa)d\kappa=0,\ \forall w\in\big\{J,JA_{1}\big\},\ g^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Take
g
to be the form of
g_{1}\kappa^{\prime}g
in (
5.53
) and integrate over
\kappa^{\prime}\in I_{p},
one then has

\displaystyle\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}wI_{p}}\varphi(g\kappa)d\kappa=\int_{I_{p}\bigsqcup I_{p}wI_{p}}\int_{I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(g_{1}\kappa^{\prime}g\kappa)d\kappa^{\prime}d\kappa=0,\ \forall w\in\big\{J,JA_{1}\big\}.

Then one can apply a similar induction argument to the proof of Lemma
5.10
to deduce that

(5.54)

\varphi(g)=\Xi_{p}(g_{1})\Xi_{p}(g).

We then take
g=g_{0}
and let
g_{1}
vary, obtaining

(5.55)

\Xi_{p}(g_{0})\Xi_{p}(g_{1})=\varphi(g_{0})=\int_{I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(g_{1}\kappa g_{0})d\kappa=\int_{I_{p}}(\pi_{p}(\kappa g_{0}))\Xi_{p}(g_{1})d\kappa.

Note by our assumption,
\Xi_{p}(g_{0})\neq 0.
Hence, from (
5.55
) we obtain

\displaystyle\Xi_{p}(\bullet)=\frac{1}{\Xi_{p}(g_{0})}\int_{I_{p}}\pi_{p}(\kappa g_{0})\Xi_{p}(\bullet)d\kappa\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}.

Therefore,
{\mathcal{V}}\subseteq{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},
a contradiction! So
(\pi_{p},{\mathcal{V}})
is irreducible.
Let

\Pi_{p}=\operatorname{Ind}_{P(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(|\cdot|_{p}^{1},1,|\cdot|_{p}^{-1})

be the induced representation. Then the functions
f
on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which belong to the space of
\Pi_{p}
are precisely the functions
G({\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
-finite on the right which satisfy

f(pg)=\delta_{P}(p)f(g).

Hence the function
\varphi_{f}
defined in Lemma
5.10
belongs to the space of
\Pi_{p}
and therefore
\pi_{p}
is an irreducible component of
\Pi_{p}
.
The classification of irreducible admissible representations on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),
implies that
\pi_{p}
"is" the Steinberg representation :
\pi_{p}\simeq\mathrm{St}_{p}
.

Since
\Xi_{p}
is
I_{p}
-invariant and the space of
I_{p}
-invariant vectors (the space of local new-vectors) in the Steinberg representation has dimension
1
, there exists a nonzero constant
\lambda
such that
\xi_{p}=\lambda\cdot\Xi_{p}.
It remains to
compute the matrix coefficient of
\Xi_{p}
. Let
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).
Then

\langle\pi_{p}(g)\Xi_{p},\Xi_{p}\rangle_{p}=\int_{\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\Xi_{p}(g_{1}g)\overline{\Xi}_{p}(g_{1})dg_{1}=\\
\frac{1}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\int_{\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\overline{\Xi}_{p}(g_{1})dg_{1}\int_{I_{p}}\Xi_{p}(g_{1}\kappa g)d\kappa.

Then by (
5.52
) and (
5.54
) (noting that
\Xi_{p}
is
L^{2}
-integrable by (
5.44
)), we deduce that

(5.56)

\langle\pi_{p}(g)\Xi_{p},\Xi_{p}\rangle_{p}=\frac{\Xi_{p}(g)}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\int_{\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\Xi_{p}(g_{1})\overline{\Xi}_{p}(g_{1})dg_{1}=\frac{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\cdot\Xi_{p}(g),

which proves (
5.51
).
∎

5.8.
The local period at
p=N

In this section we use the results of the previous section to establish (
5.23
).

We recall that
p=N
is inert in
E
, that
\pi_{p}
is the Steinberg representation and that
\pi_{p}^{\prime}
is a (tempered) unramified principal series representation.

Let
\xi_{p}
and
\xi_{p}^{\prime}
be local new vectors, of
\pi_{p}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{p}
respectively. We will show that

(5.57)

\Bigg|L_{p}(\pi_{p},\pi^{\prime}_{p})\cdot{\mathcal{P}}_{p}^{\natural}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})-\frac{p-1}{p^{2}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{3(1-p^{-2})}{p}\cdot\frac{p-1}{p^{2}}.

This implies (
5.23
) as well as (
5.24
) since the local factors at
1/2
and
1
do not vanish ((
5.9
)) and are of the shape
1+o(1)
as
p
becomes large.

Write
\pi_{p}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ind}\chi_{p}^{\prime}
and let

\gamma_{p}^{\prime}=\chi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{1}^{\prime})

where

A_{n}=\operatorname{diag}(p^{n},p^{-n}),\ n\geq 0.

By Macdonald’s formula (cf.
[
Mac71
]
or
[
Cas80
]
) and Lemma
A.1
, we have

(5.58)

\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{n})\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}=\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})\gamma_{p}^{\prime n}-(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-n-1}}{p^{n}\big[(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})-(1-p^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}_{p})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1}\big]}.

By Lemma
A.5
, Lemma
A.6
, Proposition
5.11
, Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geq 0}\sum_{w^{\prime}\in W_{n}^{\prime}}(-p)^{-\lambda^{\prime}(i(w^{\prime}))}p^{\lambda^{\prime}(w^{\prime})}\cdot\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{n})\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}\cdot\mu(I_{p}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-p^{-2}}{p+1}+\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{n})\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}\cdot\frac{2p^{-2n}-p^{-2n+2}-p^{-2n-2}}{p+1},

where the last equality follows from the fact that
\xi_{p}^{\prime}
is spherical. Therefore,

(5.59)

\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}=\frac{p-1}{p^{2}}-\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(A_{n})\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}\cdot\frac{(p-1)^{2}(p+1)}{p^{2n+2}}.

Since
\pi^{\prime}_{p}
is tempered, then
|\gamma_{p}^{\prime}|=1.
Hence, we obtain

(5.60)

\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{(1-p^{-1}\overline{\gamma_{p}^{\prime}})\gamma_{p}^{\prime n}-(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime})\overline{\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}^{n+1}}{p^{3n}\big[(1-p^{-1}\overline{\gamma_{p}})-(1-p^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}_{p})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1}\big]}=\frac{\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}{p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}-\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime})\overline{\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}^{2}}{p^{3}-\overline{\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}}}{(1+p^{-1})(1-\overline{\gamma_{p}^{\prime}})}.

Substituting (
5.58
) and (
5.60
) into (
5.59
) one then obtains

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}=\frac{p-1}{p^{2}}-\frac{(p-1)^{2}(p+1)}{p^{2}}\cdot\frac{\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}{p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}-\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-2}}{p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1}}}{(1+p^{-1})(1-\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})}.

A straightforward simplification shows that

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}{p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}-\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-2}}{p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1}}}{(1+p^{-1})(1-\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})}=

\displaystyle\frac{p^{3}\gamma_{p}^{\prime}(1+\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1}+\gamma_{p}^{\prime-2})-p^{2}-1-p^{-1}}{(p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime})(p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})(1+p^{-1})}.

In conjunction with
|\gamma_{p}^{\prime}|=1
we then conclude, when
p\geq 3,
that

(5.61)

\Bigg|\frac{\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}{p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime}}-\frac{(1-p^{-1}\gamma_{p}^{\prime})\gamma_{p}^{\prime-2}}{p^{3}-\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1}}}{(1+p^{-1})(1-\gamma_{p}^{\prime-1})}\Bigg|\leq\frac{3p^{3}+p^{2}+1+p^{-1}}{(p^{3}-1)^{2}(1+p^{-1})}\leq\frac{3}{p^{3}}.

Therefore, we have by (
5.61
) that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{{\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\xi_{p},\xi_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{p})}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle_{p}\cdot\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}-\frac{p-1}{p^{2}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{3(p-1)^{2}(p+1)}{p^{5}}=\frac{3(1-p^{-2})}{p}\cdot\frac{p-1}{p^{2}}.

Thus, (
5.57
) follows.
∎

5.9.
The matrix coefficient of the Steinberg representation for
U(W)

In this section, we assume that
p=N^{\prime}
is
split
.
Let
\mu^{\prime}
be a Haar measure on
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
Denote by
W_{0}^{\prime}=\big\{\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime},J^{\prime}\big\},
where
\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime}
is the identity in
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).
For
n\geq 1,
set

W_{n}^{\prime}=\big\{A_{n},J^{\prime}A_{n},A_{n}J^{\prime},J^{\prime}A_{n}J^{\prime}\big\}.

Let

W^{\prime}:=\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}W_{n}^{\prime}.

By Lemma
A.3
and the Cartan decomposition we have

\displaystyle G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})A_{n}G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\bigsqcup_{w^{\prime}\in W^{\prime}}I_{p}^{\prime}w^{\prime}I_{p}^{\prime}.

By Lemma
A.1
one has

\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}J^{\prime}I_{p}^{\prime})=p\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}).

Moreover, by Lemma
A.2
, for
n\geq 1,
one has

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n}I_{p}^{\prime})=p^{2n}\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}),\ \mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}J^{\prime}A_{n}I_{p}^{\prime})=p^{2n-1}\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n}J^{\prime}I_{p}^{\prime})=p^{2n+1}\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}),\hbox{ and }\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}J^{\prime}A_{n}J^{\prime}I_{p}^{\prime})=p^{2n}\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}).

Then for
w^{\prime}\in W^{\prime},
there exists a unique integer
\lambda^{\prime}(w^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}w^{\prime}I_{p}^{\prime})=p^{\lambda^{\prime}(w^{\prime})}\mu(I_{p}^{\prime}).

In particular,
\lambda^{\prime}(\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime})=0,

\lambda^{\prime}(J^{\prime})=1;
and for
n\geq 1,

\lambda^{\prime}(A_{n})=2n,\ \lambda^{\prime}(J^{\prime}A_{n})=2n-1,\ \lambda^{\prime}(A_{n}J^{\prime})=2n+1\hbox{ and }\lambda^{\prime}(J^{\prime}A_{n}J^{\prime})=2n.

Let
\Xi_{p}^{\prime}
be the
I_{p}^{\prime}
-bi-invariant function on
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
defined by

\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(w^{\prime})=(-p)^{-\lambda^{\prime}(w^{\prime})}\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}),\ w^{\prime}\in W^{\prime}.

Then by similar analysis as that in §
5.7
we have a counterpart of Proposition
5.11
:

Proposition 5.12
.

Let notation be as before. Let
\mathrm{St}_{p}^{\prime}
be the Steinberg representation of
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. The function
\Xi_{p}^{\prime}
is a matrix coefficient of
\mathrm{St}_{p}^{\prime}
. Precisely, let
\xi_{p}^{\prime}\neq 0
be a local new vector in
\mathrm{St}_{p}^{\prime}
then

(5.62)

\frac{\langle\pi_{p}^{\prime}(g)\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}\rangle_{p}}=\frac{\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(g)}{\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime})},\quad\forall\ g\in G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

5.10.
The local period at
p=N^{\prime}

In this section we deal with the case
v=p=N^{\prime}
is a split prime and establish (
5.25
) and (
5.26
).

We recall that in this case, we have the identifications

G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\simeq\GL_{3}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),\ K_{p}\simeq\GL_{3}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}})

and

G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\simeq\GL_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),\ K^{\prime}_{p}\simeq\GL_{2}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

Moreover, the subgroup
G^{\prime}\subset G
is identified with the subgroup of
\GL_{3}
leaving invariant the second element of the canonical basis:

(5.63)

\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}a&&b\\
&1&\\
c&&d\end{pmatrix}

We recall that
\pi^{\prime}_{p}\simeq\mathrm{St}^{\prime}_{p}
is the Steinberg representation and we denote its new vector by
\xi_{p}^{\prime}
; the representation

\pi_{p}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}\chi_{p}

is a tempered unramified principal series induced from a unitary character
\chi_{p}=\chi
of the diagonal torus. We denote by
\xi_{p}
is a nonzero spherical vector and by
\Xi_{p}
its associated matrix coefficient:

\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(g_{p})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle}=\frac{\Xi_{p}(g_{p})}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}.

Finally we set

\tilde{\xi}_{p}:=\pi_{p}(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})\xi_{p}

where we recall that

\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{-1}&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}=w^{\prime}.{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}.w^{\prime},\ {\mathfrak{n}}_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}1&&p^{-1}\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix},\ w^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}1&&\\
&&1\\
&1&\end{pmatrix}

Our aim is to compute the normalized period

(5.64)

{\mathcal{P}}^{*}(\widetilde{\xi}_{p},\widetilde{\xi}_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime}):=\int_{{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(g_{p})\widetilde{\xi}_{p},\widetilde{\xi}_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\pi^{\prime}_{p}(g_{p})\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}{\langle\widetilde{\xi}_{p},\widetilde{\xi}_{p}\rangle_{p}\langle\xi^{\prime}_{p},\xi^{\prime}_{p}\rangle_{p}}dg_{p}

We have

\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(g_{p})\widetilde{\xi}_{p},\widetilde{\xi}_{p}\rangle}{\langle\widetilde{\xi}_{p},\widetilde{\xi}_{p}\rangle}=\frac{\langle\pi_{p}(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}_{p}g_{p}\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle}{\langle\xi_{p},\xi_{p}\rangle}=\frac{\Xi_{p}(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}_{p}g_{p}\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}

so that

(5.65)

{\mathcal{P}}^{*}(\widetilde{\xi}_{p},\widetilde{\xi}_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime})=\int_{G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}\frac{\Xi_{p}(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}_{p}g_{p}\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(g_{p})}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime})}dg_{p}

by Proposition
5.12
.

Remark 5.7
.

Observe that since
w^{\prime}\in K_{p}
we have

\Xi_{p}(\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}_{p}g_{p}\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})=\Xi_{p}(w^{\prime}.{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}_{p}.w^{\prime}.g_{p}.w^{\prime}.{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}.w^{\prime})=\Xi_{p}({\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}_{p}.w^{\prime}.g_{p}.w^{\prime}.{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})

and for
g_{p}\in G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

w^{\prime}.g_{p}.w^{\prime}=g^{\prime}_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b&\\
c&d&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}

which the usual embedding of
\GL_{2}\hookrightarrow\GL_{3}
.
For the rest of this section and to simplify notations, we will use this later embedding in place of (
5.63
). This will allow us to replace
\tilde{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}
by
{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}
in all our forthcoming computation.

Let

w=\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}\in K^{\prime}_{p}\hbox{ and }I_{p}^{\prime}\simeq\big\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\GL_{2}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}),\ c\in p{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\big\}

be the Iwahori subgroup of
G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.

For
(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},
we set

A_{m,n}=\begin{pmatrix}p^{m}&\\
&p^{n}\end{pmatrix}\in G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}).

By the Iwahori-Cartan decomposition one has

G^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=G_{1}^{\prime}\bigsqcup G_{2}^{\prime},

where

(5.66)

G_{1}^{\prime}:=\bigsqcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n,n}\bigsqcup I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n,n}wI_{p}^{\prime}\right)

and

(5.67)

G_{2}^{\prime}:=\bigsqcup_{m\geq n+1}\left(I_{p}^{\prime}A_{m,n}I_{p}^{\prime}\bigsqcup I_{p}^{\prime}wA_{m,n}I_{p}^{\prime}\bigsqcup I_{p}^{\prime}A_{m,n}wI_{p}^{\prime}\bigsqcup I_{p}^{\prime}wA_{m,n}I_{p}^{\prime}\right).

From (
5.64
), we have

(5.68)

{\mathcal{P}}^{*}(\widetilde{\xi}_{p},\widetilde{\xi}_{p};\xi_{p}^{\prime},\xi_{p}^{\prime})=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\int_{G_{j}^{\prime}}\frac{\Xi_{p}({\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}g_{p}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(g_{p})}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime})}dg_{p}.

Let

(5.69)

I_{p}^{\prime}(1)\simeq\bigg\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\GL_{2}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}):\ c,a-1\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\bigg\}\subset I^{\prime}_{p}.

We have

{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}a&b&\\
c&d&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b&(a-1)/p\\
c&d&c/p\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}

so that

{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}I_{p}^{\prime}(1){\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\subseteq K_{p}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n,n}}\frac{\Xi_{p}({\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}g_{p}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(g_{p})}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime})}dg_{p}=\frac{\mu(I_{p}^{\prime}(1))}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\sum_{\delta}\Xi_{p}\left({\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}A_{n,n}\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\right),

where
\delta
runs over
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
.

We will compute the above integral depending on
n
: for this we notice that

1.

For
n\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}A_{n,n}\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\delta.p^{n}&&(\delta p^{n}-1)/p\\
&p^{n}&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\in K_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p^{n+1}&&\\
&p^{n}&\\
&&p^{-1}\end{pmatrix}K_{p}.

2.

For
n\leq-1
we have similarly

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}A_{n,n}\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\in K_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p&&\\
&p^{n}&\\
&&p^{n-1}\end{pmatrix}K_{p}.

3.

For
n=0
and
\delta\neq 1\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}A_{n,n}\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\in K_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p&&\\
&1&\\
&&p^{-1}\end{pmatrix}K_{p}.

From the above discussion we obtain that

(5.70)

\int_{I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n,n}}\frac{\Xi_{p}({\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}g_{p}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(g_{p})}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime})}dg_{p}=\Sigma_{01}+\Sigma_{02}+\Sigma_{03},

where

\displaystyle\Sigma_{01}:=

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}(1))}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\Bigg[\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})+(p-2)\Xi_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p&&\\
&1&\\
&&p^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\Bigg],

\displaystyle\Sigma_{02}:=

\displaystyle\frac{(p-1)\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}(1))}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\sum_{n\geq 1}\Xi_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p^{n+1}&&\\
&p^{n}&\\
&&p^{-1}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{03}:=

\displaystyle\frac{(p-1)\mu^{\prime}(I_{p}^{\prime}(1))}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\sum_{n\leq-1}\Xi_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p^{n+1}&&\\
&p^{n}&\\
&&p^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Regarding the integral along
I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n,n}wI_{p}^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{I_{p}^{\prime}A_{n,n}w^{\prime}I_{p}^{\prime}}\frac{\Xi_{p}({\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}g_{p}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(g_{p})}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})\Xi_{p}^{\prime}(\textbf{1}_{p}^{\prime})}dg_{p}=-\frac{\mu(I_{p}^{\prime}(1))}{\Xi_{p}(\textbf{1}_{p})}\sum_{\delta}\Xi_{p}\left({\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}w\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\right),

where
\delta
runs over
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}.

We will compute the above integral depending on the value of
n
: for this we observe that

1.

For
n\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}A_{n,n}w\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\delta p^{n}&&\delta p^{n-1}\\
&p^{n}&-p^{-1}\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\in K_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p^{n+1}&&\\
&p^{n}&\\
&&p^{-1}\end{pmatrix}K_{p}.

2.

For
n\leq-1
we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}A_{n,n}w\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\in K_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p&&\\
&p^{n}&\\
&&p^{n-1}\end{pmatrix}K_{p}.

3.

For
n=0
we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}^{-1}w\begin{pmatrix}\delta&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}\in K_{p}\begin{pmatrix}p&&\\
&1&\\
&&p^{-1}\end{pmatrix}K_{p}.

Thus from the above discussion we then obtain

(5.71)
